**Description of the Library Bookplate:**
A circular bookplate for the Library of Hermetic Philosophy <note>original Latin: "Bibliotheca Philosophica Hermetica"</note>. The border contains the Latin text "Hermetic Philosophy" <note>original: "PHILOSOPHIA · HERMETICA ·"</note>. The central emblem depicts a "pelican in its piety"—a traditional symbol of a bird feeding its young with blood from its own breast—nested above a blue shield bearing four red roses arranged in a cross. <note>In alchemy and Christian mysticism, the pelican represents the sacrifice of the heart and the nurturing of spiritual life; the roses on the cross are a primary symbol of the Rosicrucian tradition.</note>
</insert>

(2919) <note>Likely an acquisition or item reference number.</note>

q <note>This single letter often denotes the "quarto" size format of a book or a specific shelf location.</note>

67/1/460 <note>Archival shelfmark or cataloguing code.</note>

<term>Philosophia Hermetica</term> <note>Hermetic Philosophy: A tradition based on the writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, dealing with alchemy, astrology, and theurgy.</note>
<term>Pelican in its piety</term> <note>An iconographic motif where a mother pelican wounds her breast to feed her chicks, symbolizing self-sacrifice and the revitalization of the soul.</note>

### Germania. 1 8 1 8.
<note>The original German "Germanien" refers to the German-speaking lands in a historic or poetic sense, common in the early 19th century.</note>

<term>Trumpets and Woes</term> <note>References to the Book of Revelation (Apocalypse), where seven trumpets announce various plagues and judgments, the last three of which are called "Woes."</note>
<term>70 Danielic weeks</term> <note>A reference to a prophecy in the Book of Daniel (9:24-27) often used by theologians to calculate the timing of the Messiah's arrival and the end times.</note>
<term>Glassy Sea</term> <note>A celestial image from Revelation 4:6 and 15:2, symbolizing the purity and vastness of God's presence.</note>
<term>Gog and Magog</term> <note>Biblical figures or nations representing the final enemies of God who are defeated at the end of time.</term>

<summary>This is the title page of Christian Armbruster's 1818 treatise on biblical prophecy, specifically focusing on the timing of the apocalypse and the Second Coming of Christ. It lists a variety of mystical and prophetic topics found in the Books of Daniel and Revelation, which the author intends to prove through scripture.</summary>

<keywords>Christian Armbruster, eschatology, Book of Revelation, 666, Mark of the Beast, Millennial Kingdom, Second Coming, Danielic weeks, Gog and Magog, 19th-century theology</keywords>

By 1837 we are to expect the highest goal; then he will purge his threshing floor and gather the wheat into his barn. <note>The "highest goal" refers to the Second Coming of Christ. The imagery of the threshing floor and wheat is a biblical metaphor for the Final Judgment, where the "wheat" (the righteous) is separated from the "chaff" (the unrighteous).</note>

The ax is already laid to the root of the trees; therefore, every tree that does not bear good fruit is cut down and thrown into the fire. Matthew, Chapter 3, verses 10 and 12.

Whoever does not believe in the Kingdom of God—which will certainly begin in 1837, and which the Lord Christ himself preached through parables and images 1800 years ago, but is now moving toward perfection—shuts it against people (see Matthew, Chapter 23 in its entirety) and will be late, like the foolish virgins. Matthew, Chapter 25. <note>The "foolish virgins" refers to a parable in which several bridesmaids were unprepared for the bridegroom’s arrival and were thus excluded from the wedding feast—a metaphor for being spiritually unprepared for the end of the world.</note>

Binnigheim, in May 1813. <note>Binnigheim is a village in south-western Germany. The date 1813 indicates this text was written several years before the 1818 publication of this edition.</note>

<meta>This page follows the initial scriptural citations and the author's dating of the Millennial Kingdom to 1837. <page-type>preface</page-type>
<sig>A 2</sig>

<image-desc>A rectangular woodcut illustration showing seven angels positioned among clouds. Each angel is holding and blowing a long, straight trumpet directed downwards and towards the right. Beneath the angels, rays of light emanate from a central point at the bottom of the frame.</image-desc>

# Preliminary Warning.
<note>The original German "Vorerinnerung" can be translated as a preface, but in a prophetic context, it carries the weight of a formal "warning" or "admonition" to the reader.</note>

## Concerning the near and impending second coming of our Lord Jesus Christ.
<note>original: "zweyten Zukunft." In 19th-century theological German, "Zukunft" (literally "future") was the standard term for the "Advent" or "Coming" of Christ.</note>

### Clearly proven from the Prophets, the Evangelists, and the Holy Revelation of John.

Especially, however, concerning the seven trumpets of God <note>In the Book of Revelation, the sounding of seven trumpets by seven angels signals a sequence of apocalyptic events and divine plagues leading to the final judgment.</note>, which currently call to us from heaven: Repent! for the Kingdom of God has come very near; which also [herald] for us even greater and heavier divine judgments <note>original: "Strafgerichte." This refers to the concept of God's judicial punishments or trials visited upon the world to purge sin.</note>...

<summary>This page introduces the author's primary thesis: that the Second Coming of Christ is imminent, as evidenced by the "Seven Trumpets" of the Book of Revelation. It frames the contemporary era as a final window for repentance before the onset of divine trials.</summary>
<keywords>Second Coming, Revelation of John, Seven Trumpets, Repentance, Kingdom of God, Divine Judgment, Prophecy, Eschatology</keywords>

Revelation <note>original: "Offenbarung"</note> chapter 11, verse 15; Psalm 94, verses 1–2; Psalm 96, verse 10; Psalm 146, verse 10; Daniel 7, verse 14.

Nothing in the world is nobler than time; nothing is more changeable and shifting than time, and nothing is observed less than this very thing. It has its beginning and its progress, and with time it also reaches its end once more; man likewise has his appointed time, both for the entrance and the exit of his life. Indeed, the great God Himself descends from eternity into time, and for a while departs from time again, and returns to us in an eternal time.

<meta>This page continues the "Preliminary Warning," transitioning from a meditation on time to an urgent call for spiritual alertness. It focuses on the restoration of the "Image of God" in humanity as the 6,000-year cycle of history—aligned with the six days of creation—nears its conclusion.</meta>

Oh, it is a great wisdom to recognize the current, very important time, since we certainly have the ultimate time—indeed, the greatest world-change and transformation—imminently before us. Psalm 192, verse 27 <note>original: "Ps. 192. a. 27." This is likely a scribal or transcription error for Psalm 102:26-27, which describes the heavens and earth wearing out like a garment and being changed by God.</note>; yes, it is also a time when man can become eternally happy or unhappy.

If there ever was a time for warning (so that the greatest portion of humanity is not eternally lost, especially through the worship of the beast and its image), then it is our current, very imminent time; but if there has also ever been a time to hold out the hope of better times to the sorrowful in <term>Zion<gloss>In this context, Zion refers to the "true Church" or the community of faithful believers rather than a specific geographic location.</gloss></term> for their encouragement, and to lift them up with such hope, then it is our present, actual time, since we are indeed near to misery, but also near to help.

For Zion, or the true Church, has the hope—after its great <term>tribulations<gloss>From the German "Drangsalen," referring to a period of intense suffering or persecution expected before the end of the world.</gloss></term>, once the <term>weeds are separated from the good wheat<gloss>A reference to the parable in Matthew 13:24–30, where the "sons of the kingdom" are finally distinguished from the "sons of the evil one."</gloss></term>—of regaining the state and the "image" once more, as it was before the fall of Adam; for on the sixth day of the world's creation, Adam was created in the image of God (Genesis, chapter 1, verse 7) <note>original: "1. Buch. Mose 1. 7." The text cites 1:7, but the creation of man in God's image is recorded in Genesis 1:26–27.</note>, which Adam and Eve soon lost again through the serpent (Genesis, chapter 3). And in the sixth <term>millennium<gloss>The author follows a "chiliastic" or millenarian view of history, where each of the six days of creation corresponds to 1,000 years of human history, leading to a final seventh "Sabbath" of rest.</gloss></term>—that is, in the sixth thousand-year day of the world—we shall receive it back; for in every day of creation, during the making of the world (of which there are six), the essential thing was always introduced at that time,

<summary>The author argues that humanity is entering the final stage of a 6,000-year historical cycle, which will culminate in the restoration of the "Image of God" lost at the Fall. He balances an urgent warning against apocalyptic spiritual dangers with a message of hope for the "true Church" in Zion.</summary>

...what happens throughout the entire world in every millennium; and the main point is, if one reads the <term>six days of creation<gloss>The author follows a common millenarian tradition where the six days of work in Genesis represent 6,000 years of human history, followed by a 1,000-year "Sabbath" or Kingdom of God.</gloss></term> in the 1st Book of Moses <note>original: "1 Buch Mose" (the book of Genesis)</note> in the first chapter with reflection, then one will find everything clearly.

Since we have currently already come far into the sixth millennium, or into the sixth day, and have no more than a quarter [of it] remaining, there is hope that the lost **image of God** will soon be found again. Paul also testifies to this in 1 Corinthians, chapter 15, verse 49: just as we have long borne the image of the earthly <term>world-man<gloss>From the German "Weltmenschen," referring to humanity in its fallen, material, or non-spiritual state.</gloss></term>, so shall we also bear the image of the heavenly <term>God-man<gloss>From the German "Gottmenschen," referring to the restored, spiritual state of humanity modeled after Christ.</gloss></term>. And in the very next verses toward the end, he speaks again: we shall not all sleep until Christ comes, but we shall all be changed in a moment—not, however, that we receive immortal bodies, but rather we receive only another heart, mind, and spirit. "I will pour out my Spirit upon all flesh in the last days," <note>original: "Joel am 3. Cap, v. 1." In many German Bibles of the period, the prophecy of the Spirit found in Joel 2:28–32 in English Bibles is numbered as Joel 3:1–5.</note>; all of this happens with the **coming of Christ** into His kingdom.

King Saul, and afterwards David, 1 Samuel 10:1, and so on until our own time.

But what does God say to Samuel? They have not rejected you, but me; well then, I gave them a king in my anger, and will take him away again in my wrath, Hosea chapter 13, verse 11, which we shall experience once more in our days.

Thus Christ shall again become the King of his people, from now on and for all eternity. Revelation 11:15; Daniel 7:14; Micah 4:7; Psalm 94:1; Psalm 96:10, 13; <note>original: "2. B. Mose 15. v. 18." The Second Book of Moses is known in English as Exodus.</note> Exodus 15:18.

Whoever now considers this present, very important time and the great and heavy <term>divine judgments<gloss>From the German "Strafgerichte," referring to the trials and punishments sent by God to purify humanity before the end times.</gloss></term> which, according to the Holy Revelation of Jesus Christ, will follow one after another—if the salvation of himself, his children, and near relatives is close to his heart—then the important question arises: how will it go for me and mine? How will we still pass through this <term>Antichristian<gloss>The author is referring to the "Antichristische Zeit," a period of spiritual peril and deception expected to precede the Second Coming.</gloss></term> and dangerous time without the loss of our souls? Or what heavy tribulations, even regarding physical sustenance, will we still have to endure? But alas, this very important time is recognized by so few, and the great majority does not believe in it at all. Hence arises the lament of the great God himself through the prophet Jeremiah in chapter 8, verse 7: "Even the stork <note>The text breaks off here; the reference is to Jeremiah 8:7, which compares the migratory instincts of birds, who know their "appointed times," to people who do not recognize the judgment of the Lord.</note>

And the eternal Son of God also says: "You hypocrites, you can judge the appearance of the sky, but can you not also judge the signs of these times?" Matthew 16, verse 3. Here, he certainly means not only the scribes and <term>Pharisees<gloss>A Jewish sect described in the New Testament; here used as a symbol for religious leaders who fail to see spiritual truths.</gloss></term> of that day, but also those in our own days.

Furthermore, Christ says: "Jerusalem! Jerusalem—you **Christendom**—how often I have wanted to gather you, as a hen gathers her chicks under her wings, but you would not; therefore behold, your house of God and your dwelling place shall be left unto you desolate." Matthew 23, verses 37, 38. <note>The author explicitly interprets "Jerusalem" as a stand-in for the Christian world of his own time.</note>

From the year 1813 until 1836, we currently have a period of 23 years remaining; then it will no longer be far from the ultimate goal. In this short period, the very greatest <term>divine judgments<gloss>From the German "Strafgerichte," referring to the trials and punishments sent by God to purify humanity before the end times.</gloss></term> of God, which are surely promised in the Prophets and the Holy Revelation, will be carried out over the whole world, but especially over Christendom and over France. <note>The focus on France is typical of early 19th-century apocalyptic writing, which viewed the French Revolution and the Napoleonic Wars as divine punishment or signs of the end times.</note>

<meta>This page continues the specific chronological breakdown of the "end times" period that the author believes began in 1813, providing a year-by-year schedule of apocalyptic events based on his interpretation of the Books of Daniel and Revelation.</meta>

According to the prophecy of the Holy <term>Revelation<gloss>The Book of Revelation, the final book of the New Testament, which describes the end of the world and the return of Christ.</gloss></term>, the current minor periods beginning with the year 1816 are the most important as long as the days of heaven endure upon the earth; that is, from 1816 to 22, to 28, from 28 to 33, and from 33 to 1837.

These are called "times" in the Holy Revelation and by the prophet Daniel, and each time consists of 6 years, as we will hear hereafter. These are the <note>original: "viertehalb." In archaic German, this means three and a half (the fourth is only half).</note> three and a half last times of Daniel in Chapter 7, verse 25, and Revelation 12, verse 14, which determine for us the <term>Second Coming of Christ<gloss>The belief that Jesus will return to Earth to judge the living and the dead.</gloss></term> and shortly precede it; they are very important.

In the first period or time—that is, until 1816—we have to expect the <term>Abomination of Desolation<gloss>A biblical phrase referring to a sacrilegious presence in the temple; here, the author likely refers to the disruption of traditional church life during the Napoleonic era.</gloss></term> and the complete loss of our worship, as well as 1,260 prophetic days and the "flight of the woman into the wilderness" to France (Revelation 12, verses 6 and 14). All of this lasts for 21 years.

In the second time, up until 1822, we are to expect the worship of the beast and its image and the <term>mark of the beast<gloss>From Revelation 13; a sign required for commerce that indicates allegiance to the "Beast" or Antichrist.</gloss></term>; so that no one can buy or sell unless he has the mark, or the name of the beast, or the number of its name.

In the third time, until 1828, we Christians—those of us still alive—have a 10-year...

<summary>The author outlines a specific timeline for the apocalypse, dividing the years 1816 to 1837 into "times" of six years each, during which he expects the Abomination of Desolation and the rise of the Mark of the Beast.</summary>

## Three Woes.

<meta>This page continues the author's discussion of the prophetic timeline, specifically focusing on the "solemn oath of God" mentioned at the end of page 49. The author is calculating the exact duration of the "times" remaining before the Second Coming, specifically looking toward the year 1836.</meta>

...that henceforth there shall be no more time until the completion of the mystery of God—or more precisely, until the second coming of Christ—but rather, in the days of the voice of the seventh angel, when he shall sound his trumpet, then the mystery of God shall be fulfilled, as he has declared to his servants and prophets.

Now the important question is whether this divine oath has already occurred, or whether it is still in the future; or even, when it will occur. The late Dr. Bengel <note>Johann Albrecht Bengel (1687–1752) was a famous Lutheran clergyman and scholar whose chronological calculations of biblical prophecy, particularly his prediction that the Millennium would begin in 1836, deeply influenced 19th-century German pietism.</note> asserts that it already occurred in the year 800.

<margin>1</margin>
Now the question is, what "no more time" means. It has already been proven in this booklet that a "time" <note>In prophetic interpretation, a "time" is often calculated as a year of years or a specific block of time based on the book of Daniel.</note> consists of 7 years—that is: 6 years of labor and one Sabbath year. It has also been shown that we are currently in the last three and a half times <note>original: "viertehalb." An archaic German counting term meaning "the fourth is a half," or 3.5.</note>, which are written of in the Prophet Daniel in chapters 7 and 12, and in Revelation in chapter 12. These began in the year 1811 and end with the year 1836. However, since Satan rules these three and a half times and possesses great power within them, God the Lord has taken away Satan’s Sabbath years and allowed him only the 6 years of labor, as was already mentioned above. Consequently, a "time" no longer consists of 7 years, but only of 6 years; and thus these three and a half times no longer begin in the year 1812, but rather with the year 1816, and end in 1836.

<summary>The author interprets the "end of time" not as the end of the world, but as a change in how prophetic "times" are calculated, shortening them from seven years to six by removing the Sabbath years during Satan's final reign. This adjustment aligns the author's timeline with the year 1836, a date famously predicted by the scholar J.A. Bengel for the start of the Millennium.</summary>

## Concerning the Number 666 as the Mark of the Beast.

Now I come to the number 666, which is written in the Book of Revelation in the 13th chapter, in the last verse, and is very important in these last days and is approaching its fulfillment.

Now, this is a very important number; it has great significance, and yet it is a rejected number by God Himself. It has neither title nor name, even though all other numbers also have a title or name; they are accompanied by either days, or weeks, or months, or years, or times. But with this number, nothing stands beside it; rather, it is only the number of a single man, or the number of his name. "Here is wisdom," writes John, "let him who has understanding consider <note>original: "überleget" — can mean to reflect, ponder, or calculate.</note>, or calculate the number of the beast; it is 666."

The late <note>original: "selige" — literally "blessed," a term of respect for the deceased.</note> Doctor Bengel <note>Johann Albrecht Bengel (1687–1752) was a famous Lutheran Pietist and biblical scholar whose chronological calculations regarding the end times were widely influential in the 18th and 19th centuries.</note> took great pains with this number, as one finds in his writings. He interpreted it entirely in terms of the papal power and authority, which was supposed to last for 666 years, beginning with Pope Hildebrand <note>Gregory VII (reigned 1073–1085), one of the most powerful and reform-oriented popes of the Middle Ages.</note> in the year 1080 and ending in our days. However, this opinion is incorrect, and the description also contradicts it; for the text clearly says it is only one man's number, and not the number of many men, because in 666 years many Popes could have reigned, especially since they are elected at an advanced age.

<vocab>Number 666, Mark of the Beast, Revelation, Doctor Bengel, Pope Hildebrand, Papal Power</vocab>

<summary>The author discusses the prophetic number 666, arguing that it refers to a specific individual rather than a centuries-long institution like the Papacy as argued by Johann Bengel.</summary>
<keywords>666, Book of Revelation, Mark of the Beast, Johann Albrecht Bengel, Pope Gregory VII, Hildebrand, eschatology, papal authority, biblical interpretation</keywords>

And so, in this very same 12th chapter from verses 7 to 12, the fall of the dragon—who was cast from heaven to the earth—is a representation of the Roman Pope, who was cast from the Church heaven (that is, from Rome to France) <note>This likely refers to the events of the French Revolutionary Wars; in 1798, French troops under Napoleon's direction occupied Rome and took Pope Pius VI into captivity in France.</note> onto the earth. And his angels, that is: his servants, will also have been cast there. However, his "short time," which is written of in the 12th verse of this chapter, which the Pope—or more properly, the <term>Papacy</term><note>The office or institution of the Pope.</note>—has left to live, begins with the year of Christ 1794 and ends with the year 1836; a period of 42 years. At that time, the Papacy—or more properly, the false church—will come to an eternal end, and then a completely new, pure, and true Church will be established, which will no longer have weeds among the good wheat <note>A reference to the Parable of the Tares in Matthew 13:24–30, where "weeds" or "unrated" represent the wicked hidden among the righteous.</note>.

Now follow the seven last plagues, which are poured out through the seven bowls of wrath, for with them the wrath of God comes to an end; they are written in the 15th and 16th chapters of the Holy <term>Revelation</term><note>The final book of the New Testament, also known as the Apocalypse of John.</note>, and will reach their fulfillment shortly before the coming of the Lord in the year 1836 and make short work of it.

Now, the 7 bowls of wrath are directed solely at the despisers of God and Christ—that is: at the worshippers of the beast and its image, and those who have accepted the mark; but primarily also at the throne of the beast, for his kingdom was darkened by the fifth [bowl]... <note>Revelation 16:10: "And the fifth angel poured out his vial upon the seat of the beast; and his kingdom was full of darkness."</note>

And the dead in Christ will rise first; then we who are alive and remain shall be caught up together with them to meet the Lord, and so we shall always be with the Lord. And this is the **thousand-year kingdom**<term>Millennium: Based on Revelation 20, this refers to a thousand-year period where Christ and the resurrected saints rule before the final end of the world.</term>.

Here, Paul clearly describes the **First Resurrection**, for he says that when the Lord Christ descends from heaven, the dead in Christ will rise first. He says: not all the dead, but only the dead in Christ. However, on the **Last Day**, all the dead shall rise, as may be seen in John, Chapter 1, verse 28, and in verse 25 just before it, where the first resurrection is also clearly stated.<note>The author cites John 1:25 and 28, but the biblical context suggests he is referring to John 5:25 and 5:28, which discuss the dead hearing the voice of the Son of God.</note>

And Paul describes the two resurrections clearly once more in 1 Corinthians, Chapter 15, in verses 22, 23, and 24, saying that the first resurrection concerns only those who belong to the Lord Christ.

And these are the ones who, in Chapter 6, verses 9, 10, and 11, are heard under the altar at the opening of the **fifth seal**; they are also written of in Chapter 11, verse 18, and in Chapter 20, verse 4. They are nothing but holy **martyrs**, who are also [described] in Chapter 7, verses 9 and 14...

### Concerning the Coming of Christ.

Now, concerning the certainty of the second **Coming of Christ**<note>original: "Zukunft Christi." While "Zukunft" means "future" in modern German, in this 18th/19th-century religious context, it specifically refers to the Advent or Second Coming.</note>, I wish to report something from the Evangelists, which also belongs among the hidden mysteries of God; and though these may appear to be insignificant things, they are nevertheless pure divine truths.

We find the **Ascension of Jesus Christ** described twice by the **Evangelist Luke**: first, in his Gospel in the 24th chapter, and second, in the **Acts of the Apostles** in the 1st chapter.

And in each description, there is a longing on the part of the disciples to know the distance<note>original: "Entfernung." This likely refers to the "remoteness" or the interval of time until the restoration of the kingdom.</note> of the **Kingdom of Israel**, or of Christ; but He, as the **Searcher of Hearts**<term>Searcher of Hearts: A translation of "Herzenskündiger," a common pietist term for God or Jesus, emphasizing His ability to know the innermost thoughts and timing that humans cannot yet grasp.</term> (who well knew that it would have been of no use to His disciples and their descendants until this present day), never revealed it to them openly. However, He did always place it secretly into each chapter where they questioned Him, by means of specific numbers.

<summary>The author predicts that the Second Coming of Christ will occur in November 1837 and argues that Christ hid the timing of His return within the numerical structure of the New Testament.</summary>
<keywords>Second Coming, 1837, Evangelist Luke, Ascension, Acts of the Apostles, Searcher of Hearts, Kingdom of Israel, biblical numerology</keywords>

Now, as for the mystery of the second **Coming of Christ**<note>original: "Zukunft Christi." As previously noted, this refers to the Advent or return of Jesus.</note>, which **Luke** describes in his 24th chapter (wherein he also describes the **Resurrection and Ascension of Christ**), it is this: in this chapter, in the 13th verse, the number 60 is found, which describes the distance of a remote place named **Emmaus** from Jerusalem. Along this road, two disciples were walking on the very first day after the Resurrection of our Lord Jesus Christ, and they spoke with one another regarding the Lord Christ’s suffering and death. And it happened that while they were so speaking, Jesus drew near to them and spoke with them, and walked with them until the 30th verse, and he interpreted the Scriptures for them. As they drew near to the village, they urged him to stay with them, and he went in with them; and it happened that as he sat at the table with them and broke the **bread**, they recognized him, and he vanished from before them. This, then, was our Lord Christ’s first appearance after his Resurrection, and it stands written in **Luke, chapter 24, from the 13th to the 30th verse**.

## Proofs of the Coming of Christ through the 6 <term>Cronus</term> <note>original: "Cronus"; likely derived from the Greek *Chronos*, referring here to specific epochs or units of prophetic time used for calculation.</note>.

Furthermore, we find the second coming of the Lord Christ determined twice more, and specifically by a beloved disciple, John, in the 21st chapter of his Gospel—not to mention what this John writes of this matter in his Revelation. In regarding that Book of Revelation, one should perhaps apply oneself with somewhat greater diligence to understanding it, because in our days it is of the highest necessity to know, as almost the entire Revelation is reaching fulfillment in these last days.

However, because many do not believe in the Holy Revelation at all, but instead reject it—and indeed do so because they do not understand it—they will certainly also have no part in the great glory written therein, which will be revealed in these last days; nor will they believe what John writes in his Gospel concerning the second coming of Christ.

For in the Revelation, we find the second coming of Christ determined not only once; we find it determined through 6 Cronus, or times, with each Cronus or time amounting to 300 <note>The text breaks off here at the end of the page; the author is beginning to explain a system where history is divided into six periods of 300 years each to calculate the end of the world.</note>

XII

<meta>This page continues the author’s discussion of the "two witnesses" mentioned on the previous page. Then, by way of parable <note>original: "Gleichnißweise"; an analogy used to explain a spiritual truth.</note>, whenever an earthly, worldly king wishes to take possession of a kingdom—whether he has obtained it by way of inheritance or by the sword—he also sends his emissaries ahead into that same new kingdom. He has them proclaim his imminent arrival and the taking of his realm. Without any doubt, the emissaries are sent to the capital and royal city of that kingdom, so that when the new king arrives and wishes to take possession of his kingdom, he finds everything prepared. His subjects <note>original: "Unterthanen"; those living under the rule of a monarch.</note> then await him with great joy and reverence, especially when the subjects know that they are expecting a gracious, merciful, and kind king who will make them happy not only temporally <note>original: "zeitlich"; meaning during their physical life on earth.</note> but also eternally. Because he also delivers them from much and great misery <note>original: "Jammer"; a term for deep distress or suffering.</note>, the subjects will certainly await their king with great longing and with joy.

And these two emissaries were certainly also two favorites of the king; otherwise, he would not have entrusted them with this honor and power to prepare his way to his kingdom. After this preparation, they will also have great honor to expect from the king; without doubt, they will also sit at his right and left hand in his kingdom, and will rule and reign with him as long as the king and his kingdom endure.

And so it is also with the two witnesses of Christ, whom the Lord Christ also sends beforehand...

# 122

And I saw an angel flying through the midst of heaven, who had an eternal Gospel to proclaim to those who sit and dwell upon the earth, and to all nations and tribes and languages and peoples, and spoke with a great voice: Fear God, and give Him the glory, for the hour of His judgment is come; and worship Him who made heaven and earth, and sea, and the springs of water <note>This is a translation of Revelation 14:6-7, a central passage in Reformation and post-Reformation theology often used to identify specific historical movements or figures as divine messengers.</note>.

In this description we have five points to consider.

**First:** what kind of heaven this is, through which this angel flew.

**Second:** what the "midst of heaven" is, through which this angel flew.

**Third:** what kind of angel this is, who flew through the midst of heaven.

**Fourth:** what the time of divine judgment is, which this angel proclaims.

**Fifth:** why this angel's Gospel is called an eternal Gospel.

Of these five points, only a little will be reported.

**First:** what kind of heaven this is, through which this angel flew.

Since the Revelation of John is a complete prophetic prophecy <note>original: "Weissagung," referring to the divinely inspired foretelling of future events</note>, and pertains exclusively until the 20th Chapter to the Church...

## A Beautiful Spiritual Song.

->Melody: Bridegroom of the Soul.<- <note>original: "Seelen Bräutigam"—a popular 17th-century pietist hymn tune by Adam Drese, often used for deeply personal and emotional spiritual texts.</note>

1. Fairest Paradise,
Which revealed itself in days of old;
When we were in a state of innocence,
Nearly six thousand years ago, <note>In the 18th and 19th centuries, many theologians followed the "Ussher chronology," which calculated the age of the world as approximately 6,000 years since creation.</note>
When Paradise
Revealed itself upon the earth.

2. Will you, beloved land,
Named Canaan,
Will you then not come again
Among the redeemed faithful,
Who through God’s hand
Are now transformed. <note>The author uses "Canaan," the biblical Promised Land, as a metaphor for the coming Millennial Kingdom or the restored Eden.</note>

And the curse,
Which now covers the earth
And terrifies all of humanity?
Only Satan’s night
Has brought the curse.

Soon be brought to ruin?

A light comes once again,
That breaks through everything.

So that the end becomes again
As the beginning was on earth;
Paradisiacal and mild,
After the first image. <note>The author expresses "Chiliasm" or Premillennialism—the belief that the world will return to its original, perfect state before the final judgment.</note>

6. Now it is indeed certain
That no Paradise
Is to be found on earth anymore;
Yet the misery shall vanish,
And there surely comes
Another Paradise.

Rise up from the sleep of darkness, and put on the **armor of light.** <note>original: "Waffen des Lichts"—a reference to Romans 13:12, encouraging believers to cast off "works of darkness" and prepare for the "day" of salvation.</note>

Up, up, you fellow citizens of the kingdom, your King is approaching! Receive the great Man of Wonders without wearying; he comes on white horses—he, as a Prince of Victory. He gathers his flocks; his name is Jesus Christ. **Revelation, Chapter 19, verses 11 and 14.** <note>These verses describe a vision of heaven opening and a rider on a white horse, followed by the armies of heaven on white horses, representing Christ’s triumphant return.</note>

# Appendix

->regarding the<-

## seven last Trumpets and Woes, etc.

Because the <term>thousand-year kingdom</term><note>Commonly known as the Millennium, a prophesied period of peace and holiness on Earth before the final judgment.</note> or Sabbath, which will certainly begin by 1837 <note>The specific date of 1837 suggests the author was influenced by the calculations of Johann Albrecht Bengel (1687–1752), a prominent pietist theologian.</note>, is believed by so few people—even though it is just as certain as the fact that we currently have a Sabbath every week (though not for much longer)—certainly the great <term>World-Week</term><note>The "Chiliastic" belief that human history spans 7,000 years: 6,000 years of labor/conflict followed by a 1,000-year "Sabbath" of rest.</note>, which consists of 7,000 years, also has its thousand-year Sabbath. Therefore, to provide certainty on this matter, just a small portion of it will be explained here.

If one asks nearly all spiritual scholars today whether the glorious description by Ezekiel from chapters 40 to 48 (which belongs precisely to this great glory in the thousand-year Sabbath) was fulfilled by Joshua and Zerubbabel <note>Leaders of the Jewish people who returned from the Babylonian Captivity to rebuild the Temple in Jerusalem (the Second Temple).</note> as mentioned in Haggai chapter 2; however, when one reads through the aforementioned 2nd chapter of Haggai, if one has even a slight insight into the matter, one will certainly find that a third temple is still to be built, which will have far greater glory than the first two.

<summary>The author argues for the reality of a coming 1,000-year "World Sabbath" beginning in 1837, claiming that Ezekiel's prophecies point toward a future third temple rather than past historical events.</summary>
<keywords>Millennium, Sabbath, World-Week, Ezekiel, Haggai, Third Temple, 1837, Zerubbabel</keywords>

--- Page 152 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's mathematical and prophetic analysis of the biblical book of Ezekiel, specifically linking the detailed measurements of the Temple to the "World Sabbath" or thousand-year kingdom discussed on the previous page.</meta>

Surely, the temple in Ezekiel was not described in such great detail for no reason, nor was everything else contained in that description. Therefore, this glorious description in Ezekiel remains entirely unfulfilled at present, and belongs completely to the <term>thousand-year kingdom</term><note>A prophesied 1,000-year period of Christ's reign on Earth, often referred to as the Millennium.</note> of Christ here on Earth. God the Lord Himself placed this into the description through hidden numbers, but He kept it concealed until our own time, though it is now being unlocked.

It has already been indicated in this booklet that the number 40 is indispensable for unlocking the <term>mysteries of God</term>. For this reason, the description begins with the 40th chapter.

Right at the beginning of the description, in the first verse, we find the number 25 mentioned, and right above it, the number 40 is designated as the chapter. If one multiplies the number 25 by the number 40, the number 1000 will be accurately represented, as can be seen here:

->2 5<-
->4 0<-
->1000 years.<-

And this confirms for us with certainty the thousand-year kingdom, and that the description of <term>Ezekiel</term><note>original: "Ezechielis", the Latin genitive form of the name Ezekiel, commonly used in scholarly German texts of this period.</note> belongs to it.

Furthermore, two other numbers appear in this first verse, namely 10 and 14. If one multiplies these as well, we find the number 140. If one then multiplies this 140 by the number 25, which stands right there with it, the number becomes 3500

# A True Proof
->of our 21-year flight, and the<-
->following 10-year captivity.<-

We read in the 3rd Book of Moses <term>The Book of Leviticus; however, the author is actually citing Exodus 12:40, which describes the 430 years the Israelites spent in Egypt</term> in the 12th Chapter, in the 40th verse, that the time that the children of Israel were on their flight and under hard service in Egypt was 430 years.

And in the 1st Book of Moses <term>The Book of Genesis</term> in the 27th Chapter, from the 41st to the 43rd verse, we read that the patriarch Jacob also fled from his brother Esau—because he wanted to strangle him on account of the blessing—to his mother's brother Laban in Haran, and served there in hard service for 20 years.

When one divides this 430-year flight of the children of Israel by the 20-year flight of Jacob, our own 21-year flight and our 10-year captivity will accurately reveal themselves as a remainder, as can be seen here <insert>$\frac{4^1 3 0}{2 0} |$ 21 years.</insert> <note>The author is using long division: 430 divided by 20 equals 21, with a remainder of 10. He interprets the quotient as a 21-year period of "flight" and the remainder as a 10-year period of "captivity."</note>
The 10 years left over signify our captivity from 1827 to 1837.

# Hymn

## Melody: I will not let go of my Jesus. <note>Original: "Meinen Jesum laß ich nicht," a popular 17th-century Lutheran hymn by Christian Keimann.</note>

1. At last, at last it must come to pass,
That distress finds its end;
At last **Esau’s** yoke shall break, <note>In this context, Esau’s yoke symbolizes the oppression or worldly power that the faithful must endure before their deliverance.</note>
At last anxiety and grief vanish;
At last the stone of sorrow
Must also be transformed into gold.

2. At last one plucks the roses,
At last one makes it through the desert;
At last the walking staff
Must prepare for the Fatherland; <note>The "Fatherland" refers to the spiritual home or the Kingdom of Heaven.</note>
At last the seed of tears brings
That which the harvest of joy possesses. <note>A reference to Psalm 126:5, "Those who sow in tears shall reap with shouts of joy."</note>

3. At last one sees **Canaan**
Lying beyond **Egypt’s** house of bondage; <note>The author uses the Exodus story—leaving slavery in Egypt for the Promised Land of Canaan—as a metaphor for the soul's journey to peace.</note>
At last one reaches **Tabor**,
When the misery has been climbed; <note>Mount Tabor is traditionally identified as the site of the Transfiguration of Jesus, representing a moment of divine glory after struggle.</note>
| At last a **Jacob** enters
Where no **Esau** shall be anymore.

4. "At last"—O you beautiful word,
You can sweeten every cross;
When the rock is pierced,
It finally lets balsam flow.
Oh my heart, therefore take note:
At last, at last, surely there comes
Still a beautiful Paradise.

---

<summary>This page contains a four-stanza hymn focusing on the theme of "At Last" (Endlich), promising that the suffering of the faithful will eventually end in divine peace. It uses biblical allegories like the journey from Egypt to Canaan and the ultimate triumph of Jacob over Esau to provide comfort.</summary>
<keywords>Hymn, deliverance, Esau's yoke, Canaan, Egypt, Tabor, Jacob, Paradise, perseverance</keywords>
